Rammage,  curate  of  Blanter,  James  Clerk  in  Auchtinraicht,  John  Clerk,  there, 
John   Wilsoune,    and   Hugh    Hammyltoune,    dweller   in   Blanter. 

249.  John  Weight.  Eodem  die  et  hora. 

John  Weycht  in  Auchtinraicht  produced  to  John  Miller,  bailie  in  that 
part,  a  precept  granted  by  John  Dunbar  of  Blanter,  charging  the  bailie  to 
give  sasine  to  John  Weicht  and  his  heirs  male,  of  the  forty  shilling  land 
of  new  extent  of  Auchtinraicht,  with  the  pertinents,  which  the  said  John  and 
his  mother  then  occupied,  lying  in  the  granter's  barony  of  Blanter,  and 
sheriffdom  of  Lanark;  to  be  held  of  the  granter  in  feu  farm  conform  to  a 
charter.  The  precept  is  subscribed  and  sealed  as  in  No.  248,  with  the 
addition  of  John  Clerk  and  James  Clerk  as  witnesses.  Sasine  given  at  the 
dwellinghouse   situated   upon   the   said   lands. 

250.  Patrick  Clerk  in  Awchtinraycht.     Eodem  die  et  Iwra. 

Patrick  Clerk,  dwelling  in  Awchtinraicht,  produced  a  precept  in  favor 
of  himself  and  his  heirs  male  (in  terms  similar  to  those  in  Nos.  248  and 
249),  of  the  two  merk  lands  of  new  extent  of  Auchtinraycht,  with  their 
pertinents,  which  the  said  Patrick  then  occupied,  lying  in  the  said  barony 
and    sheriffdom.       Sasine   given   on   the   ground    of   the  lands    as    in   No.    249. 


14  I.  1  Paul  IV.]  TOWN   CLERKS   OF   GLASGOW.  85 

David   Norvell   and   John    Weicht    are    witnesses    in    the  precept    in  place   of       ^If 
John   Clerk   and   James   Clerk. 

251.  James  Clerk  in  Auchinraycht. 

James  Clerk,  dwelling  in  Awchtinraycht,  produced  a  precept  in  favor 
of  himself  and  his  heirs  male  (in  terms  similar  to  those  in  Nos.  248-50), 
of  the  one  merk  land  of  new  extent  of  the  lands  of  Awchtinraicht,  with 
the  pertinents,  lying  in  the  said  barony  and  sheriffdom.  Sasine  given  as  in 
Nos.  248-50. 

252.  Marion  Jaksoune  and  John  Clerk,  her  Son.     Eodem  die  et  kora. 
Marion  Jaksoune  and  John  Clerk,  her   son,  produced   a   precept    (in   terms 

similar  to  those  in  Nos.  248-51)  in  favor  of  the  said  Marion  Jaksoune, 
during  her  widowhood,  and  of  John  Clerk,  and  his  heirs  male  in  fee,  of 
the  two  merk  lands  of  new  extent  of  Awchtinraicht,  with  the  pertinents 
which  the  said  Marion  then  occupied,  lying  in  the  said  barony  and  sheriffdom. 
Sasine   given   as   in   Nos.    248-51. 

253.  John  Wilsoune.  Eodem  die  et  hora. 

John   Wilsoune,    in    Auchtinraicht,    produced   a   precept    (in    terms   similar       f.  ten. 
to   those   in   Nos.     248-51)   in   favor   of    himself    and    his    heirs    male,    of    the 
two   merk   lands   of   new   extent   of  Auchtinraicht,    with   the  pertinents,  which 
he   then    occupied,  lying   in   the   said   barony  and   sheriffdom.     Sasine  given  as 
in   Nos.    248-51. 

254.  John  Craig.  Eodem  die  et  hora. 

John  Dunbar,   of   Blanter,  gave   sasine  propriis   manibus   to   John   Craig   in       f-  16L 
liferent,    and    to    John    Craig,    his    son,   heritably,    of   the    two    merk    lands   of 
Awchtinraicht,    then   occupied   by  the   first-named   grantee. 

255.  John  Chalmyr  and  Spouse.     2  December,  1555. 

Sir  Martin  Reid,  chaplain,  sold  in  feu  farm  to  John  Chalmyr  and  Marion 
Smyth,  his  spouse,  and  1.1.,  a  particate  of  land  of  the  commonty  of  the  city, 
lying  t.c.  in  the  street  leading  from  the  South  Port  to  the  bridge  of  Glasgow, 
on  the  north  side  thereof,  extending  in  length,  between  the  booth  now 
belonging  to  the  said  Sir  Martin  on  the  north  and  the  street  leading  to 
the  said  bridge  on  the  south,  five  ells,  except  the  distance  between  the  said 
street    and    the    front    of   the   ground    of   the    said    particate,    and    in    breadth 


86  PROTOCOLS   OF  THE  [a.d.  1555. 

extending  to  six  ells;  with  free  ingress  and  egress  to  and  from  the  said 
particate  of  land  on  the  east  side;  with  all  its  eavesdrops,  gutters,  or  water- 
courses, and  other  pertinents;  also  a  booth  contiguous  to  the  said  particate 
of  land  lying  between  the  lands  of  q.  James  Gibsoune  on  the  west,  the 
lands  or  common  vennel  and  lands  Of  John  Patersoune  on  the  east,  and  the 
lands  of  Richard  Ros  on  the  north;  and  that  for  payment  of  an  annualrent 
of  28s.  Sasine  given  by  David  Lyoune,  bailie.  Witnesses:  Mr.  John  Hall, 
David   Huchesoune,    David   Lauder,   John   Clwne,    junior. 

256.  Sir  Martin  Reid.  Eodem  die. 

David  Lyoune,  bailie,  gave  sasine  to  Sir  Martin  Reid  of  the  annualrent 
of   28s.,    specified   in   No.    255. 

257.  Henry  Burell.         3  December,   1555. 

Andrew  Dunlop,  burgess  of  the  burgh  of  Glasgw,  judicially  acknowledged 
in  the  court  of  Glasgow  that  he  lately  received  from  Henry  Burell,  burgess 
of  the  said  burgh,  the  sum  of  170  merks,  for  redemption  and  relief  of  a 
tenement  of  the  said  Henry,  which  had  been  wadset  by  him  to  the  said 
Andrew  upon  reversion;  which  tenement  lies  t.c.  in  the  great  street  leading 
from  the  place  of  the  Friars  Preachers  to  the  Market  Cross,  on  the  east 
side  thereof,  between  the  tenement  of  Archinbald  Blakburne  on  the  south, 
the  common  vennel  on  the  north,  the  yard  of  Mathew  Heriot  on  the  east, 
and  the  street  on  the  west;  and  the  said  Andrew  having  renounced  all 
claim  to  the  property,  David  Lyoun,  bailie,  gave  sasine  thereof  to  the  said 
Henry.  Witnesses :  David  Lyoune,  Michael  Lyndsay,  bailies ;  John  Mwre, 
John   Rob,    William   Hall,    Robert    Lyndesay. 

258.  John  Mytschell.      24  December,  1555,  11  a.m. 

Henry  Burell  sold  to  John  Mytschell,  dwelling  in  Langsyde,  the  tenement, 
with  yard  and  pertinents,  described  in  No.  257.  Sasine  given  by  David 
Lioune,  bailie.  Witnesses:  Mychaell  Lyndesay,  bailie;  John  Mwre,  John  Rob, 
William  Hall,  William  Lyndesay,  John  Wedrop,  Robert  Mytschell,  in  Mekill 
Gowane;  John  Duncane,  servitor  to  John  Kyncaide,  dyer;  John  Ride,  George 
Dunlop,    burgess;   David   Watsoune   and   Robert   Burne. 

259.  George  Clemy  and  Mergaret  Flemynge.     31  Jan.,  1555-6,  2  p.m. 
Mergaret    Flemynge    resigned   in   favor  of   herself   and    of   George  Clemy, 

junior,  her  spouse,  and  1.1.,  (1)  a  half  acre  of  land  lying  in  the  croft  called  the 


u  I.  l  Paul  IV.]         TOWN   CLERKS  OF  GLASGOW.  87 

Crubbis,  between  the  lands  of  David  Letrige  on  the  east,  and  the  lands  of 
Rammishorne  on  the  west;  (2)  a  half  acre  or  thereby,  lying  in  the  croft 
called  the  Dowhill,  between  the  lands  of  the  said  David  Letrige  and  his 
spouse  on  the  west,  the  lands  of  the  chaplainry  of  St.  Kentigern  on  the  east, 
the  lands  of  William  Eoger  on  the  south,  and  the  lands  of  the  Pedagogy  on 
the  north;  (3)  a  barn,  with  the  pertinents,  lying  between  the  lands  of 
James  Flemyng  on  the  west,  the  lands  of  the  said  Mergaret  and  David 
Letrige  on  the  east,  and  the  yard  of  James  Grahame  on  the  north;  (4)  a  rig 
of  land  in  the  croft  of  the  Pedagogy,  between  another  rig  belonging  to  the  said 
David  Letrige  on  the  north,  and  the  lands  of  James  Grahame  on  the  south; 
(5)  two  rigs  of  land  lying  in  the  said  croft,  between  other  two  rigs  of 
land  belonging  to  the  said  David  on  the  south,  and  the  lands  of  the 
chaplainry  of  All  Saints  on  the  north;  (6)  the  half  of  a  yard  and  half  of  a 
tenement  (excepting  the  fore  part)  lying  t.c.  in  the  great  street  leading  from 
the  Quadrivium  to  the  Market  Cross,  between  the  other  half  of  the  said 
yard  and  tenement  belonging  to  the  said  David  on  the  south,  the  fore 
tenement  belonging  to  the  said  David  on  the  west,  the  yard  of  q.  Patrick 
Scot,  .  .  .  and  the  ends  and  sides  of  sundry  other  yards  on  the  north. 
Sasine  given  by  David  Lyoune,  bailie.  Witnesses:  David  Lyndesay,  of 
Kittoksyide,  David  Letrig,  David  Mathy,  burgess  of  Glesgow;  James  Dalgleis, 
mair  of  fee,  viz.,  serjeant  of  the  barony  (seriando  baronie);  George  Flemynge, 
David   Watsoune,    and   Robert   Burne,    Serjeants. 

260.     George  Clemy  and  Spouse.         Eodem  die  et  hora. 

George  Clemy  resigned  in  favor  of  himself  and  Mergaret  Flemynge,  his 
spouse,  and  1.1.,  two  workshops,  lying  t.c.  in  the  street  leading  from  the  place 
of  the  Friars  Preachers  to  the  Market  Cross,  between  the  lands  of  q.  George 
Swan  on  the  south,  and  the  common  vennel  called  the  Blakfreir  Wynde  on  the 
north.     Sasine  given  by  David  Lyoune,  bailie.

scriberentur  aliquid  fraudis  presumeretur. 

[The  end  of  this  protocol,  and  of  the  first  hook  of  protocols.  I  was  unwilling  to  write  further,  hecause 
the  subsequent  leaves,  for  what  reason  I  know  not,  have  been  cancelled,  and  if  any  were  written 
upon  fraud  might  he  presumed.] 


This  buik  contenis  ane  hundreth  thre  scoir  thre  levis,  comptand  five  seoir 
for  the  hundreth,  quhilkis  ar  all  ruarkit  as  the  saniyn  beris.
